We stress that, even if these relations seem to depend on the coordinate systems, the correlation functions do not. The coordinate system defined above and represented in Fig.
5
, and these relations hold only in this system.

Explicit Computations

We want now to express these correlation functions in terms of the power spectrum
C^{\phi}_{\ell}(z_{1},z_{2})
. The computations are easily performed in the
(+,-)
basis. The main relations are (see Section
5
for more details)

\displaystyle\gamma^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{1}\pm\mathrm{i}\gamma_{2}\,,

(64)

\displaystyle\gamma^{+}(z,\boldsymbol{n})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=2,m}\phi_{\ell,m}(z)\nu_{\ell}\;{{}_{+2}\mathchoice{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}}(\boldsymbol{n})\,,

(65)

\displaystyle\gamma^{-}(z,\boldsymbol{n})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=2,m}\phi_{\ell,m}(z)\nu_{\ell}\;{{}_{-2}\mathchoice{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}}(\boldsymbol{n})\,,

(66)

\displaystyle\nu_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{(\ell+2)!}{(\ell-2)!}}\,,

(67)

\displaystyle\phi(z,\boldsymbol{n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell,m}\phi_{\ell,m}(z)\mathchoice{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}(\boldsymbol{n})\,.

(68)

Moreover, we define the functions
\tilde{P}_{\ell}(\mu)
and
\tilde{Q}_{\ell}(\mu)
as

\displaystyle\frac{2\ell+1}{16\pi}\tilde{P}_{\ell}(\mu)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \equiv\penalty\

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\frac{2\ell+1}{4\pi}}\;_{+2}\mathchoice{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.48611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.34721pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,-2}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.48611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.34721pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,-2}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.48611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.34721pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,-2}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.48611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.34721pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,-2}}}}(\theta,\pi/2)\penalty\ =\penalty\ -\sqrt{\frac{2\ell+1}{4\pi}}\;_{+2}\mathchoice{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.48611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.34721pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,-2}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.48611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.34721pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,-2}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.48611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.34721pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,-2}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.48611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.34721pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,-2}}}}(\theta,\pi/2)\,,

(69)

\displaystyle\frac{2\ell+1}{16\pi}\tilde{Q}_{\ell}(\mu)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \equiv\penalty\

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\frac{2\ell+1}{4\pi}}\;_{+2}\mathchoice{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.55719pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.55719pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.6639pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.90279pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,+2}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.55719pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.55719pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.6639pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.90279pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,+2}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.55719pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.55719pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.6639pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.90279pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,+2}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.55719pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.55719pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.6639pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.90279pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,+2}}}}(\theta,\pi/2)\penalty\ =\penalty\ -\sqrt{\frac{2\ell+1}{4\pi}}\;{{}_{-2}\mathchoice{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.48611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.34721pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,-2}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.48611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.34721pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,-2}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.48611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.34721pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,-2}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.48611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.34721pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,-2}}}}(\theta,\pi/2)}\,,

(70)

more details are given in Appendix.
3
.

We compute first the correlation function
\langle\gamma^{+}(z_{1},\boldsymbol{n}_{1})\gamma^{-}(z_{2},\boldsymbol{n}_{2})\rangle
. Recall that
\boldsymbol{n}_{1}
and
\boldsymbol{n}_{2}
are given by

\displaystyle\theta_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi}{2}\quad

\displaystyle\varphi_{1}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(71)

\displaystyle\theta_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi}{2}

\displaystyle\varphi_{2}

\displaystyle=\varphi\,.

(72)

The correlation function is given by

\displaystyle\langle\gamma^{+}(z_{1},\boldsymbol{n}_{1})\gamma^{-}(z_{2},\boldsymbol{n}_{2})\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2},m_{1},m_{2}}\langle\phi_{\ell_{1},m_{1}}(z_{1})\phi_{\ell_{2},m_{2}}(z_{2})\rangle\nu_{\ell_{1}}\nu_{\ell_{2}}\,{{}_{+2}\mathchoice{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.973pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.973pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.20505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell_{1},m_{1}}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.973pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.973pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.20505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell_{1},m_{1}}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.973pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.973pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.20505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell_{1},m_{1}}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.973pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.973pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.20505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell_{1},m_{1}}}}}}(\boldsymbol{n}_{1})\,{{}_{-2}\mathchoice{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.973pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.973pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.20505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell_{2},m_{2}}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.973pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.973pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.20505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell_{2},m_{2}}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.973pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.973pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.20505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell_{2},m_{2}}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.973pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.973pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.20505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell_{2},m_{2}}}}}}(\boldsymbol{n}_{2})

(73)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\ell}C^{\phi}_{\ell}(z_{1},z_{2})\nu_{\ell}^{2}\left(\sum_{m}{{}_{+2}\mathchoice{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}}(\boldsymbol{n}_{1})\,{{}_{+2}Y^{\star}_{\ell,m}}(\boldsymbol{n}_{2})\right)\,,

(74)

where we used the conjugation properties Eq. (
203
) and Eq. (
207
) together with the definition of the power spectrum Eq. (
209
). The sum can be done explicitly using the addition theorem for the Spin Weighted Spherical Harmonics (see Appendix
2
with
s_{1}=+2
and
s_{2}=-2
) and gives

\displaystyle\sum_{m}{{}_{+2}\mathchoice{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,m}}}}}(\boldsymbol{n}_{1})\,{{}_{+2}Y^{\star}_{\ell,m}}(\boldsymbol{n}_{2})

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{\frac{2\ell+1}{4\pi}}{{}_{+2}\mathchoice{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.48611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.34721pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,-2}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.48611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.34721pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,-2}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.48611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.34721pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,-2}}}}{Y^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11273pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.48611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.34721pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell,-2}}}}}\left(\varphi,\frac{\pi}{2}\right)

(75)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\ell+1}{16\pi}\tilde{P}_{\ell}(\mu)\,,

(76)

Combining the expressions Eq. (
74
) and Eq. (
75
), we get the final expression for the correlation function

\displaystyle\langle\gamma^{+}(z_{1},\boldsymbol{n}_{1})\gamma^{-}(z_{2},\boldsymbol{n}_{2})\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell}\frac{2\ell+1}{64\pi}C^{\phi}_{\ell}(z_{1},z_{2})\nu_{\ell}^{2}\tilde{P}_{\ell}(\mu)\,.

(77)

The other correlation functions can be computed following the same steps. We use the indices
a,b=1,2
and
\alpha,\beta=+,-
. The final result is

\displaystyle\langle\gamma^{\alpha}(z_{1},\boldsymbol{n}_{1})\gamma^{\beta}(z_{2},\boldsymbol{n}_{2})\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell}\frac{2\ell+1}{64\pi}C^{\phi}_{\ell}(z_{1},z_{2})\nu_{\ell}^{2}\,\Pi^{\alpha\beta}_{\ell}(\mu)\,,

(78)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\Pi^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\ell}}}}{\Pi^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\ell}}}}{\Pi^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\ell}}}}{\Pi^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\ell}}}}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{Q}_{\ell}(\mu)&\tilde{P}_{\ell}(\mu)\\
\tilde{P}_{\ell}(\mu)&\tilde{Q}_{\ell}(\mu)\end{pmatrix}\,.

(79)

The correlation function in the
(\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{2})
basis can be found inverting the relation Eq.
64
to express
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
as

\displaystyle\gamma_{a}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{M^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.5364pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.5364pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72462pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.089pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a\alpha}}}}{M^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.5364pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.5364pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72462pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.089pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a\alpha}}}}{M^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.5364pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.5364pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72462pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.089pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a\alpha}}}}{M^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.5364pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.5364pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72462pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.089pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a\alpha}}}}\gamma^{\alpha}\,,

(80)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{M}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
-\mathrm{i}&\mathrm{i}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(81)

With these notations, we get

\displaystyle\langle\gamma_{a}(z_{1},\boldsymbol{n}_{1})\gamma_{b}(z_{2},\boldsymbol{n}_{2})\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell}\frac{2\ell+1}{64\pi}C^{\phi}_{\ell}(z_{1},z_{2})\nu_{\ell}^{2}\,\Pi{{}_{ab,\ell}}(\mu)\,,

(82)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\Pi^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.11821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.11821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.05411pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.32434pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ab,}{\ell}}}}{\Pi^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.11821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.11821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.05411pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.32434pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ab,}{\ell}}}}{\Pi^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.11821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.11821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.05411pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.32434pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ab,}{\ell}}}}{\Pi^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.11821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.11821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.05411pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.32434pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ab,}{\ell}}}}(\mu)=\mathchoice{M^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.5364pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.5364pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72462pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.089pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a\alpha}}}}{M^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.5364pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.5364pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72462pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.089pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a\alpha}}}}{M^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.5364pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.5364pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72462pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.089pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a\alpha}}}}{M^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.5364pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.5364pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72462pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.089pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a\alpha}}}}\mathchoice{M^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05107pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05107pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.87447pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.48177pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b\beta}}}}{M^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05107pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05107pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.87447pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.48177pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b\beta}}}}{M^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05107pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05107pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.87447pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.48177pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b\beta}}}}{M^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05107pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05107pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.87447pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.48177pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b\beta}}}}\mathchoice{\Pi^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\ell}}}}{\Pi^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\ell}}}}{\Pi^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\ell}}}}{\Pi^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\ell}}}}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{P}_{\ell}(\mu)+\tilde{Q}_{\ell}(\mu)&0\\
0&\tilde{P}_{\ell}(\mu)-\tilde{Q}_{\ell}(\mu)\end{pmatrix}\,.

(83)

Final Results

Recall that these computations were done in the preferred coordinate system with both galaxies at the Equator. In this system, the invariant correlation functions are given by Eq. (
61
) and Eq. (
62
). The final expression can be obtained using Eq. (
82
), which yields

\displaystyle\zeta_{\mathrm{p}}(z_{1},z_{2},\mu)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell}\frac{2\ell+1}{128\pi}C^{\phi}_{\ell}(z_{1},z_{2})\nu_{\ell}^{2}\left(\tilde{P}_{\ell}(\mu)+\tilde{Q}_{\ell}(\mu)\right)\,,

(84)

\displaystyle\zeta_{\mathrm{c}}(z_{1},z_{2},\mu)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell}\frac{2\ell+1}{128\pi}C^{\phi}_{\ell}(z_{1},z_{2})\nu_{\ell}^{2}\left(\tilde{P}_{\ell}(\mu)-\tilde{Q}_{\ell}(\mu)\right)\,.

(85)

It is tempting to define

\zeta_{\pm}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\zeta_{\mathrm{p}}\pm\zeta_{\mathrm{c}})\,,

(86)

to get

\displaystyle\zeta_{+}(z_{1},z_{2},\mu)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell}\frac{2\ell+1}{128\pi}C^{\phi}_{\ell}(z_{1},z_{2})\nu_{\ell}^{2}\tilde{P}_{\ell}(\mu)\,,

(87)

\displaystyle\zeta_{-}(z_{1},z_{2},\mu)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell}\frac{2\ell+1}{128\pi}C^{\phi}_{\ell}(z_{1},z_{2})\nu_{\ell}^{2}\tilde{Q}_{\ell}(\mu)\,.

(88)

Finally, using the orthogonality relation for the functions
\tilde{P}_{\ell}(\mu)
and
\tilde{Q}_{\ell}(\mu)
given in Appendix
3
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{+1}\zeta_{+}(z_{1},z_{2},\mu)\tilde{P}_{\ell}(\mu)\,\mathrm{d}\mu

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}C^{\phi}_{\ell}(z_{1},z_{2})\nu_{\ell}^{2}\,,

(89)

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{+1}\zeta_{-}(z_{1},z_{2},\mu)\tilde{Q}_{\ell}(\mu)\,\mathrm{d}\mu

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}C^{\phi}_{\ell}(z_{1},z_{2})\nu_{\ell}^{2}\,.

(90)

The relations Eq. (
87
), Eq. (
88
), Eq. (
89
) and Eq. (
90
) are the main theoretical results of this Section. They relate the invariant correlation functions, which are observable, to the power spectrum of the lensing potential, which is a theoretical quantity predicted by the specific cosmological model at hand.

3
Estimator for the Correlation Functions

We want now to define an estimator, using observable quantities, to quantify the invariant correlation functions
\zeta_{\pm}
. The logic goes as follows. For two fixed redshifts, we consider a couple of galaxies: One located at
(z_{1},\boldsymbol{n}_{1})
and the other located at
(z_{2},\boldsymbol{n}_{2})
, with
\boldsymbol{n}_{1}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}_{2}=\mu
. With these galaxies, we build the preferred coordinate system as explain in the previous Section. We then measure the scaled rotation of each galaxy and define

\Xi\equiv\Theta(z_{1},\boldsymbol{n}_{1},\beta_{1})\Theta(z_{2},\boldsymbol{n}_{2},\beta_{2})\,,

(91)

where
\beta_{i}
is the angle between the semi-minor axis of galaxy
i
and the first basis vector
\boldsymbol{e}_{1}
.

However, we have two independent correlation functions to estimate, and hence we need two different observables: Another couple of galaxies located at
(z_{1},\boldsymbol{n}_{1}^{\prime})
and
(z_{2},\boldsymbol{n}_{2}^{\prime})
separated by the same angular difference
\boldsymbol{n}_{1}^{\prime}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}_{2}^{\prime}=\mu
and located at the same redshifts is needed. From this couple, we define a second observable as

\Xi^{\prime}\equiv\Theta(z_{1},\boldsymbol{n}_{1}^{\prime},\beta_{1}^{\prime})\Theta(z_{2},\boldsymbol{n}_{2}^{\prime},\beta_{2}^{\prime})\,.

(92)

Using the expression for the scaled rotation

\Theta(z,\boldsymbol{n},\beta)=\gamma_{2}(z,\boldsymbol{n})\cos 2\beta-\gamma_{1}(z,\boldsymbol{n})\sin 2\beta\,,

(93)

its two-point correlation function (in the preferred coordinate system) is

\langle\Theta(z_{1},\boldsymbol{n}_{1},\beta_{1})\Theta(z_{2},\boldsymbol{n}_{2},\beta_{2})\rangle=\zeta_{+}\cos\left((2(\beta_{1}-\beta_{2})\right)+\zeta_{-}\cos\left(2(\beta_{1}+\beta_{2})\right)\,,

(94)

where we used the expression for
\langle\gamma_{1}\gamma_{1}\rangle
and
\langle\gamma_{2}\gamma_{2}\rangle
given by Eq. (
61
) and Eq. (
62
), and the definitions of
\zeta_{\pm}
given by Eq. (
86
) (together with Eq.(
83
) to see that the cross-correlation vanishes)

We will now assume that
\Xi
and
\Xi^{\prime}
are estimators of this two-point correlation functions. In other words, we have

\displaystyle\Xi

\displaystyle=\hat{\zeta}_{+}(z_{1},z_{2},\mu)\cos\left((2(\beta_{1}-\beta_{2})\right)+\hat{\zeta}_{-}(z_{1},z_{2},\mu)\cos\left((2(\beta_{1}+\beta_{2})\right)\,,

(95)

\displaystyle\Xi^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\hat{\zeta}_{+}(z_{1},z_{2},\mu)\cos\left((2(\beta^{\prime}_{1}-\beta^{\prime}_{2})\right)+\hat{\zeta}_{-}(z_{1},z_{2},\mu)\cos\left((2(\beta^{\prime}_{1}+\beta^{\prime}_{2})\right)\,.

(96)

It is possible to solve Eq. (
95
) and Eq. (
96
) to express the estimators for the correlation functions as

\displaystyle\hat{\zeta}_{+}(z_{1},z_{2},\mu)

\displaystyle=\Xi\,F_{1}(\beta_{1}^{\prime},\beta_{2}^{\prime},\beta_{1},\beta_{2})+\Xi^{\prime}\,F_{1}(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{1}^{\prime},\beta_{2}^{\prime})\,,

(97)

\displaystyle\hat{\zeta}_{-}(z_{1},z_{2},\mu)

\displaystyle=\Xi\,F_{2}(\beta_{1}^{\prime},\beta_{2}^{\prime},\beta_{1},\beta_{2})+\Xi^{\prime}\,F_{2}(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{1}^{\prime},\beta_{2}^{\prime})\,,

(98)

and the explicit expression for the functions
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
are found in Appendix
18.C
. Note that in the expressions for the estimators Eq. (
97
) and Eq. (
98
) there is no dependence on the various angles
\beta
. It is a slight abuse of notation, but the idea is that for each galaxy quadruplet, we obtain one estimator for
\zeta_{+}
and one for
\zeta_{-}
. Even if each of these individual quantities depends on the angles
\beta
, their final average should be independent on the galaxies orientation and only depend on the angle
\varphi
between the pixel and the redshifts. Finally, we see that by construction, these estimators satisfy

\langle\hat{\zeta}_{\pm}(z_{1},z_{2},\mu)\rangle=\zeta_{\pm}(z_{1},z_{2},\mu)\,,

(99)

in other words the estimators Eq. (
97
) and Eq. (
98
) are unbiased.

4
Error Estimation

General Idea

Let us recap now what should be done in practise to estimate the correlation functions
\zeta_{\pm}(z_{1},z_{2},\mu)
, together with their errors. For each galaxy, one measures

•

its position in the sky
\boldsymbol{n}
;

•

its redshift
z
;

•

the rotation angle
\delta\alpha
between its major-axis and the direction of the polarisation of light;

•

the position of the semi-minor axis of the galaxy
\beta
;

•

its eccentricity.

Note that the position of the semi-minor axis
\beta
can only be measured when a couple of galaxies has been chosen, which defines the preferred coordinate system.

Each of these quantities is measured with a given error. An estimator is thus given by
2
2

2

We drop the plus/minus index for clarity, the logic being the same for both estimators.

\hat{\zeta}_{i}
with an associated error
\Delta\hat{\zeta}_{i}
, where
i
represents one estimator (associated with a given quadruplets of galaxies). Even is the individual errors are high (e.g. of the same order or even higher than the signal itself), it is still possible to obtain a precise enough estimator, see
[
6
]
. To achieve this goal, we need to construct the best estimator from several individual estimators.

Best Estimator

With a given catalogue, we build a set of estimators
\hat{\zeta}_{i}
, the number of which depends on the particular catalogue. From this set, we define the best estimator with weights
w_{i}
as

\hat{\zeta}^{\mathrm{BE}}=\sum_{i}w_{i}\hat{\zeta}_{i}\,,

(100)

and its error is

(\Delta\hat{\zeta}^{\mathrm{BE}})^{2}=\sum_{i}w_{i}^{2}\Delta\hat{\zeta}_{i}^{2}\,.

(101)

The weights satisfy
\sum_{i}w_{i}=1
and their values is given imposing the condition that the
SNR
(
SNR
), defined as

\mathrm{SNR}=\frac{\hat{\zeta}^{\mathrm{BE}}}{\Delta\hat{\zeta}^{\mathrm{BE}}}\,,

(102)

is maximal. It is straightforward to find the solution which is
3
3

3

We do not use Einstein summation convention here.
(see
[
81
]
for more details)

\displaystyle w_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Z}\frac{1}{\hat{\zeta}_{i}\tau_{i}^{2}}\quad\mathrm{with}

(103)

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\frac{1}{\hat{\zeta}_{i}\tau_{i}^{2}}\quad\mathrm{and}

(104)

\displaystyle\tau_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta\hat{\zeta}_{i}}{\hat{\zeta}_{i}}\,.

(105)

In this case, the
SNR
is

\mathrm{SNR}=\sqrt{\sum_{i}\frac{1}{\tau_{i}^{2}}}\,.

(106)

Remark 3.7
(
Divergent estimators
)
–

Note that, from the expressions Eq. (
95
) and Eq. (
96
), it is clear that some values for the angles may lead to divergent values for the estimators, for example if
\beta_{1}-\beta_{2}\sim\beta_{1}^{\prime}-\beta_{2}^{\prime}\sim\pi/4
, the value of
\hat{\zeta}_{+}
will be arbitrarily high. However, the error on this estimator will be also high. When we compute the best estimator, the weights
w_{i}
associated with such measurements will be close to zero, and in the end, those
ill-defined
pairs will not contribute to the final result.

Note that, in practise, this is not an easy task. For example, with a catalogue with
N_{\mathrm{g}}\sim 10^{6}
galaxies, it is possible to extract
N_{\mathrm{e}}\sim 10^{24}
estimators (from the number of quadruplets). Assuming a computing power of
10^{9}
(!) operations per second, computing
\zeta^{\mathrm{BE}}
would take roughly
$10^{15}\text{\,}\mathrm{s}$\sim$10^{8}\text{\,}\mathrm{yrs}$
, which is not very doable.

Without a precise catalogue and real data at hand, we cannot go much further. To extract some physical insights, we will make the following assumptions: All the relative errors
\tau_{i}
satisfy

\tau_{i}\lesssim\tau_{0}\,,

(107)

where
\tau_{0}
is a constant quantifying the maximum relative error. In this case, the
SNR
given by Eq. (
106
) is

\mathrm{SNR}\gtrsim\frac{\sqrt{N_{\mathrm{e}}}}{\tau_{0}}\,,

(108)

where
N_{\mathrm{e}}
is the number of estimators at hand, which we wish now to estimate.

Number of Estimators

We want to count the number of estimators we can extract to estimate
\zeta(z_{1},z_{2},\mu)
. First, we estimate the number of couples of galaxies subtending an angle
\varphi
with
\mu=\cos\varphi
. We consider angular pixels of aperture
\delta\theta
. The solid angle spanned by these pixels is given by (at lowest order in
\delta\theta
)

\delta\Omega=\delta\theta^{2}\pi\,.

(109)

The total number of pixels is then

N_{\mathrm{p}}=f_{\mathrm{sky}}\frac{4\pi}{\delta\Omega}=\frac{4f_{\mathrm{sky}}}{\delta\theta^{2}}\,,

(110)

where we assumed that only a fraction of the sky
f_{\mathrm{sky}}
is covered by the survey.

Once such a pixel has been chosen as
first pixel
, we need to count the number of pixels subtended by an angle
\varphi
around it. Assuming that the first pixel is fixed at the North Pole, the solid volume forming an angle
\varphi\pm\delta\theta/2
is given by

\delta\Omega_{\varphi}=2\pi\int_{\varphi-\delta\theta/2}^{\varphi+\delta\theta/2}\;\sin\theta\,\mathrm{d}\theta=2\pi\delta\theta\sin\varphi\,.

(111)

In this volume, the number of pixels is given by

N(\varphi)=\frac{\delta\Omega_{\varphi}}{\delta\Omega}=\frac{2\sin(\varphi)}{\delta\theta}\,.

(112)

Finally, the number of couples of pixels separated by an angle
\varphi
is given by

N_{\mathrm{c}}(\varphi)=\frac{1}{1+\delta_{z_{1},z_{2}}}N_{\mathrm{p}}\times N(\varphi)=\frac{1}{1+\delta_{z_{1},z_{2}}}\frac{8f_{\mathrm{sky}}\sin\varphi}{\delta\theta^{3}}\,.

(113)

The fraction with the Kronecker delta prevents over-counting if we consider auto-correlation (
z_{1}=z_{2}
).

In a given pixel at redshift
z
, the number of observed galaxies is
N_{\mathrm{g}}(z)
. Hence, the total number of pairs of galaxies is

N_{\mathrm{p}}(z_{1},z_{2},\varphi)=N_{\mathrm{g}}(z_{1})N_{\mathrm{g}}(z_{2})N_{\mathrm{c}}(\varphi)\,.

(114)

Finally, an estimator is built with two pairs of galaxies. The number of estimators is then given by

\displaystyle N_{\mathrm{e}}(\varphi,z_{1},z_{2})\simeq\frac{1}{1+3\delta_{z_{1},z_{2}}}\frac{1}{2}\left(N_{\mathrm{g}}(z_{1})N_{\mathrm{g}}(z_{2})\frac{8f_{\mathrm{sky}}\sin\varphi}{\delta\theta^{3}}\right)^{2}\,,

(115)

where we assumed that the number of galaxies is sufficiently high to assume
N_{\mathrm{p}}-1\approx N_{\mathrm{p}}
, and we used that
\delta_{z_{1},z_{2}}^{2}=\delta_{z_{1},z_{2}}
.

As explained in the previous section, if
N_{\mathrm{e}}
is the number of estimators, the
SNR
would scale as
\sqrt{N_{\mathrm{e}}}
(see Eq. (
106
)). However, the underlying assumption is that all the estimators (and their errors) are independent from each other. This is certainly not always the case: If galaxies are observed in the same pixels, the errors should be somehow related. For example, if there are strong magnetic fields in a given direction, the polarisation direction measured from all these galaxies will carry the same error. For this reason, a more pessimistic assumption would be that the number of estimators is simply the numbers of pairs of pixels

\displaystyle N_{\mathrm{e}}(z_{1},z_{2},\varphi)\approx\frac{1}{1+\delta_{z_{1},z_{2}}}\frac{8f_{\mathrm{sky}}\sin\varphi}{\delta\theta^{3}}\,,

(116)

where we again assumed that the number in the brackets is sufficiently large.

5
Results

In principle, we could stop here, as an explicit catalogue of galaxies is needed to go further. However, we provide here a very simple simulated example for the correlation functions
\zeta_{\pm}(z_{1},z_{2},\mu)
. We use CLASS
[
22
,
136
]
to compute the power spectrum
C^{\phi}_{z_{1},z_{2}}
, with
\ell_{\mathrm{max}}=20^{\prime}000
and the parameters taken from Planck
[
5
]
are

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle=0.6781\,,

\displaystyle h^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{cdm}}

\displaystyle=0.0552278\,,

\displaystyle h^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{b}}

\displaystyle=0.0102921\,,

(117)

\displaystyle\log(10^{9}A_{\mathrm{s}})

\displaystyle=0.742199\,,

\displaystyle n_{\mathrm{s}}

\displaystyle=0.9660499\,,

\displaystyle T_{\mathrm{CMB}}

\displaystyle=$2.7255\text{\,}\mathrm{K}$\,.

(118)

In principle, one should compute the sums given by Eq. (
87
) and Eq. (
88
) to compute the correlation functions. However, the functions
\tilde{P}_{\ell}
and
\tilde{Q}_{\ell}
oscillate violently (especially for large
\ell
) which makes numerical computations difficult. Instead, we can use the flat sky approximation which is valid for small angles, see
[
14
,
66
,
122
,
113
,
30
,
200
,
201
,
18
]
or Appendix
18.D
for more details. The correlation functions are then given by

\displaystyle\zeta_{+}(z_{1},z_{2},\varphi)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\;\ell^{5}\,J_{0}(\ell\varphi)\,\frac{1}{4}C^{\phi}_{\ell}(z_{1},z_{2})\,\mathrm{d}\ell\,,

(119)

\displaystyle\zeta_{-}(z_{1},z_{2},\varphi)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\;\ell^{5}\,J_{4}(\ell\varphi)\,\frac{1}{4}C^{\phi}_{\ell}(z_{1},z_{2})\,\mathrm{d}\ell\,,

(120)

where
J_{n}(x)
is the Bessel function of order
n
. Moreover, we take

\displaystyle f_{\mathrm{sky}}

\displaystyle\approx 0.7\,,

(121)

\displaystyle\delta\theta

\displaystyle=5^{\prime}\approx 1.4\times 10^{-3}\,,

(122)

\displaystyle\tau_{0}

\displaystyle\approx 10^{3}\,,

(123)

inspire from the values of SKA2
[
29
]
. This yields

\mathrm{SNR}\approx\frac{40}{\sqrt{1+\delta_{z_{1},z_{2}}}}\sqrt{\sin\varphi}\,.

(124)

The results for all the correlations with
z=1,\,1.5,\,2
are shown in Fig.
7
. This correspond to light emitted respectively
8\text{\,}\mathrm{Gyrs}
,
9.5\text{\,}\mathrm{Gyrs}
and
10.5\text{\,}\mathrm{Gyrs}
ago, i.e. when the rate of stars formation was high. The results agree with
[
122
]
. We see that the correlation functions is not very sensitive to the redshift, i.e. the result are similar, with less than one order of magnitude of difference.
